# Correspondence between the paper and the Lean formalization

This table maps every numbered statement and display of S. Armstrong and V. Vicol, *Anomalous regularization of passive scalars up to the Obukhov–Corrsin exponent* ([doc/paper.pdf](paper.pdf)), to the Lean declarations that prove it, or says why none is needed (figures, attributions, heuristics). Statement, equation, section, figure and table numbers are those of the paper. The main results are described in the [README](../README.md).

## How to read the tables

- One table per section of the paper, Sections 1 to 15, then the places where the text says more than an exposed Lean statement. Every statement of the paper has exactly one row; numbered displays that only continue a proof are covered by the row of the statement or proof that contains them.
- **Lean declarations** are given by name, without their namespace (`AVRegularity`, `AVRegularity.PaperFacing`, `AVRegularity.Providers`, …), each followed by the file that declares it, relative to the repository root. Declarations of the formalization of [AV25] (the dependency, namespaces `AVenhance` and `Homogenization`) are marked as such.
- **Kind**:
  - *direct*: the Lean statement is the paper's, up to notation and packaging;
  - *definition*: the Lean definition is the paper's;
  - *weakening*: the paper's statement follows from the Lean's by specializing a parameter, weakening the conclusion, strengthening a hypothesis or comparing norms; the note says how;
  - *composition*: an elementary composition of the Lean statements listed (an induction, a chain of inequalities, arithmetic on the definitions), with no declaration of its own;
  - *see rows*, *explanatory*, *attribution*, *none*: a row without a declaration of its own (a reference to other rows; a heuristic or idea of proof that asserts nothing beyond the statements it cites; a statement about another work; a figure).
- The paper writes $`\theta_{\mathsf{in}}`$ and $`t_{\mathsf{in}}`$ for the initial datum and the initial time; the Lean statements name them `f` and `a`.
- Where the paper bounds a norm in $`L^\infty`$ in time, the Lean statement usually gives the bound at every time, for a representative; the notes say so with the words **every time**.
- Comments in the library under `AVRegularity/` cite earlier texts, among them earlier versions of this paper; their numbers are not those of this table. The comments of `AVRegularityChallenge.lean` and `AVRegularitySolution.lean` use the paper's numbers.

## Section 1. Introduction

The introduction has two numbered displays, (1.1) and (1.2), no numbered statement, and six subsections: 1.1 Main results (with the paragraphs "Construction of the velocity field", "Interior regularity", "Uniform anomalous dissipation", "Local regularity" and "Vanishing diffusivity"), 1.2 Prior results on anomalous dissipation and the Obukhov–Corrsin exponent, 1.3 Strategy of the proof, 1.4 Lean formalization, 1.5 Use of artificial intelligence and 1.6 Organization of the paper. Its mathematical claims are the statements of Sections 2 to 6 and 9, which carry the rows below; the rest attributes results of other papers.

| Paper | Lean declarations | Kind | Note |

| Abstract (the equation; a velocity field constructed as in [AV25], Hölder continuous with exponent $`\alpha\lt\frac13`$; anomalous regularization in $`C^{0,s}`$ for every $`s`$ below the Obukhov–Corrsin exponent, the field depending on $`s`$, uniformly in the bands; anomalous dissipation at a uniform rate; "Each of our main results, together with its proof, is fully formalized in Lean 4") | rows Theorem A, Theorem B (Section 3) and the "Lean formalization" rows below | see rows | the abstract restates Theorems A and B and the paragraph on the Lean formalization. The field depends on $`s`$ through $`q`$, as in the Lean roots, which fix $`\alpha,s,q,\zeta`$ before the threshold $`\Lambda_0`$, $`\Lambda`$ and the field; the main results are Theorems A, B and C and Corollary D |

## Section 2. Construction of the velocity field

The definitions of Section 2 are those of `AVRegularity/Statements/Construction` and `AVRegularity/Statements/Intermediate/CycleReference`. The Lean writes `ocTau`, `ocTauP`, `ocTauPP` for $`\tau_m`$, $`\tau'_m`$, $`h_m`$, and `ocA` for $`\varepsilon_m^{\beta-2}`$, with $`\beta=1+\alpha`$; the paper's $`\mu`$ is `ocMuS`, while `ocMu` is the rate $`4\pi^2\nu/\varepsilon_m^2`$. In Lean names, `Pipe` (as in `outerPipe`) refers to the paper's tube.

## Section 3. Main results

Theorems A, B and C and Corollary D have two Lean forms: the main results in `AVRegularity/Statements/Main` (see the [README](../README.md#the-main-results)), and the theorems of `AVRegularity/Proofs/PaperFacing/MainResults.lean`, which state them for the one velocity field of Section 2 with one $`\Lambda_0`$ (`paper_main_results`): `PaperTheoremA`, `PaperTheoremB`, `PaperTheoremC`, and `PaperCorollaryDGlobal` and `PaperLocalLimits` for Corollary D. The weak solutions and the Hölder norm of the paper are `IsWeakSolutionOn` (and `IsWeakSolutionOnGrad`, which names the gradient: `IsWeakSolutionOn b κ t₁ t₂ f θ` is `∃ Dθ, IsWeakSolutionOnGrad b κ t₁ t₂ f θ Dθ`; the paper-facing theorems write it `IsWeakSolutionOnWith` (`AVRegularity/Applications/TorusEnergy/With.lean`), with the same body) and `CsNormLe`. The Lean statements quantify over every stream sequence $`\Phi`$ of the recursion (2.12) (`IsOCStreamSeq`) and over its limit $`\mathbf b`$, and choose the constants after them; the recursion determines $`\Phi`$ by induction on $`m`$, so the constants depend only on the parameters named in the paper.

## Section 5. Iterated homogenization

Propositions 5.1 to 5.4 are stated in the Lean at one structure of parameters, `PaperParameters` (`AVRegularity/Proofs/PaperFacing/Basic.lean`), which carries the schedule of orders, the constants of the invariant, the statements of the entry and of a passage, and the parameters of the entry; `paper_parameters_exist` (`AVRegularity/Proofs/PaperFacing/Parameters.lean`), and `paper_parameters_exist_order`, which prescribes the order of the invariant, show that it exists for $`\Lambda\ge\Lambda_0`$, $`J_w\ge1`$ and $`0\lt t_0\lt T`$. The theorems that state Propositions 5.1 to 5.4 at these parameters are `paper_entry` (Proposition 5.1; `AVRegularity/Proofs/PaperFacing/EntryStatement.lean`), `paper_cascade_cycle_passage` (`AVRegularity/Proofs/PaperFacing/CascadeCyclePassage.lean`) (Proposition 5.1 and Propositions 5.2 to 5.4 at one choice of parameters) and `paper_cascade_cycle_passage_at` (`AVRegularity/Proofs/PaperFacing/CycleSupplement.lean`) (Propositions 5.2 to 5.4), and `paper_cascade_cycle_passage_order` (the same at a prescribed order). The top level $`m_0`$ of Subsection 5.3 is `paperBottom`, and the constant $`C`$ of Propositions 5.2 to 5.4 is one constant, which is allowed since each conclusion is monotone in $`C`$ and in $`m_0`$. The Lean statements are universal in the data that the paper fixes in its construction of the families, the birth law (`IsSuccessorLaw`) and the fresh kernels (`IsFreshKernel`), which exist (`paper_fresh_kernels_exist` (`AVRegularity/Proofs/PaperFacing/TruncatedVelocity.lean`)).

## Section 6. Energy estimates and paired smoothing

| Paper | Lean declarations | Kind | Note |
|---|---|---|---|

## Section 9. Reduction and proofs of the main results

Each proof here is headed "Proof of …" and cites the statement of Part I that it proves; the rows carry the number of the statement. For the tube of Theorem C the Lean's data are $`n=m+2`$, $`T=h_{m+1}`$, $`R=r_{\mathrm{tube}}`$, $`\varrho=r_{\mathrm{obs}}/r_{\mathrm{tube}}`$, $`\vartheta=8-t_0/h_{m+1}`$ and $`t_1=t_{\mathrm f}`$; $`\mathcal K`$ is `ocBandSet`, and `X` is the flow of $`\mathbf b_m=\nabla^\perp\phi_m`$ (`IsFlow (streamVel (Φ (n - 2))) X`).

| Paper | Lean declarations | Kind | Note |

## Section 10. Cell problems and the averaged flux

The Lean states the cell problems in normalized variables (unit period, unit diffusivity); the paragraph "Normalized variables" of Section 10 is the dictionary to the physical variables.

## Section 11. Admissible families

The paper's definitions are the Lean's predicates: Definition 11.1 is `IsStageInvariant` with the constants of `IsInvariantAdmissible`, Definition 11.2 is `IsPaperSeed` (with the field `seed_plan`), Definition 11.3 is `IsActualSuccessor` followed by the projection `GenStage.retained`, and Definition 11.4 is `PaperAdmissibleByDescent` (`IsPaperFamily` is the larger class that Proposition 11.5 gives). The statements of `AVRegularity/Proofs/PaperFacing/Section10.lean` are collected at one $`m_0`$ by `paper_section10_of_parameters`.

## Section 12. Smoothing within one period: Bloch wave analysis

The corrector amplitude $`\bar\varepsilon_m=\varepsilon_m(\kappa_{m-1}/\kappa_m)^{1/2}`$ is the Lean's `ocDStar`. Proposition 12.1 for one $`(t_{\mathrm{pr}},\Pi)`$ is `paper_preparation_bundle_of_parameters` (`AVRegularity/Proofs/PaperFacing/CycleSupplement.lean`).

## Section 15. Initialization at the finest levels

The orders of Section 15 are the fields of `PaperParameters`: the weak orders, the terminal schedule `term_schedule`, the moment orders `moment_orders_eq`, the trace order `Kw_floor` and the recovery order `term_floor`. At the scale $`\varepsilon_M`$ the Lean measures the weak and terminal families at the radius $`c_K\varepsilon_M`$ (`termRStar`), with a constant $`c_K\gt 0`$ of the parameters; since $`\Vert g\Vert_{H^N_{\varepsilon_M}}\le\max\{1,c_K^{-1}\}^N\Vert g\Vert_{H^N_{c_K\varepsilon_M}}`$ by (5.8), the paper's bounds at the scale $`\varepsilon_M`$ follow, with a constant that depends on $`c_K`$ and on the order.

## Where the text says more than an exposed Lean statement

In a few places the paper says more than an exposed Lean statement. Where the extra content is proved inside a Lean proof but not exposed, the entries are Definition 11.1(b2) with Lemma 11.7, Lemma 12.3 with Proposition 12.4, Subsection 15.1, Lemma 15.6, and Proposition 15.11(iv)(b3). Where a statement or a step is more general than the case that the Lean states, which is the case that is used, the entries are Lemma 8.4, the proof of Corollary D(b), Lemma 12.2 with (12.30), Proposition 12.5(c), Lemma 12.10, Lemmas 13.2 and 13.7(ii), and Lemma 13.5; in each the Lean proves the result that the statement supports, by its own route. Where the paper makes a count, fixes a constant or fixes an order of choice by hand, the entries are Proposition 10.7, Table 11.1, the path bound (after Definition 11.4) and the weight orders of Subsection 11.1, the first display of Proposition 12.5(c), the bound $`\varepsilon_{M+1}\le r_{\mathrm{w}}\le\varepsilon_M`$ of Subsection 15.1, Proposition 15.10(c) and the sentence after (15.7); the paper's count is not compared with the Lean's term by term. The last row is a reading convention.

## Facts used by name

The paper uses some standard facts by name, with one line or a citation: Grönwall's inequality, Liouville's theorem, Plancherel's identity and Poincaré's inequality on the torus, Cauchy's estimate, the inequalities of Young, Hölder and Cauchy–Schwarz, Schur's test, the Arzelà–Ascoli theorem and the diagonal argument, the Riesz representation theorem, Duhamel's formula, Galerkin approximation and the Piola identity. Where the formalization proves the instance that is used, the row names it.

## Axioms

Every theorem of `AVRegularity/Statements/Main` depends only on `propext`, `Classical.choice` and `Quot.sound`: run `python3 scripts/check-axioms.py`, which elaborates `scripts/Axioms.lean`. The library contains no `sorry` and no custom axiom.

## References

- [AV25] S. Armstrong and V. Vicol, *Anomalous diffusion by fractal homogenization*, Ann. PDE **11** (2025), Paper No. 2, [arXiv:2305.05048](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.05048); Lean formalization: [AVAnomalousDiffusion](https://github.com/scottnarmstrong/AVAnomalousDiffusion).
